# C1Connector — round 76 w1

Verdict: **named conflict — mori already states journald retention rules; mado also records the archived-file vacuum rule in a historical safety report.**
Unchecked: no fleet-wide exclusivity, ownership arbitration, source-truth adjudication, admission, or claim-score decision is made here; no build/test/checker was run.

Axes: adjacency, C1 depth, siblings. C2 freshness (9) and the frozen C1 depth (8) target remain unchanged.

## Scope and reproducibility

Enumerated **21** `*-shokunin/` directories; excluded tsukuroi; swept **20 committed sibling trees**, all valid HEADs. Five siblings matched, fifteen returned git-grep exit 1 (no matches), zero command errors. **1,199 matching lines** are retained verbatim in the full hit table below, including tracked `.ufo` evidence, source copies and fixtures. Matching archived content is not automatically active ownership.

Exact sweep command, applied concurrently once to each of the 20 enumerated sibling repositories:

```sh
git -C <absolute-repo-path> grep -nE 'journald|SystemMaxUse|journalctl --vacuum|vacuum-size|journald.conf|journal storage' HEAD -- .
```

Enumeration: tool glob `/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/*-shokunin/` (hidden included; gitignore disabled). Each row below pins the observed `git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD`. The grep was against HEAD as assigned; pins were captured immediately before it, not atomically with it. No evidence of an intervening change is asserted either way. Negative means **this exact case-sensitive pattern in tracked HEAD bytes**, not absence of all possible synonymous retention knowledge. Working-tree/untracked sibling content is excluded by contract.

## Tsukuroi province placement

`/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/PROVINCE.md:6-14`, heading **Core**, says: “systemd user units & timers ... journal/boot triage (`journalctl -b`, dmesg error classes)”. Lines 3-4 define “the OS substrate itself: package state, systemd, drivers/firmware, boot health.” Lines 38-42 add that a recipient is an analysis/report owner and shared evidence is not shared ownership.

**Placement:** journald retention is **not explicitly named** as either Core or Frontier; **[INFERENCE]** daemon/configuration diagnosis fits the existing broad systemd/journal Core, not a separately declared retention frontier. Exact local province search `journal|retention|Round.76` (case-insensitive) found journal references but no explicit retention-plane declaration. This distinction must not be rounded into an explicit charter grant.

## Contradictions and bounded proposal

The conclusion “no sibling holds the journald retention plane” is false under the assignment's operational test “a lane states a journald retention rule”: mori's committed knowledge entry does, and mado's historical safety report does. Conversely, this does **not** establish that mori owns daemon configuration or that all eight JD rows duplicate its knowledge. Mori expressly refuses that ownership. Nor does this falsify the narrower frozen claim “this lane never held” merely because a sibling held related ground.

**One proposed move:** file a pinned **retention-as-evidence-reach / retention-as-daemon-configuration boundary packet** to mori and the protocol integrator, referencing mori `journal-reach-r49.md:14,24-39,63` and tsukuroi Core above. Ask for explicit acknowledgement of shared rules versus distinct questions; cross-reference the existing mori rules as overlap, while retaining tsukuroi's distinct manager clamp/startup-min-use/per-file derivation analysis under the unchanged eight-row target. No deletion, weakened claim, ownership transfer, new gate or runtime action is proposed. Mado is an additional consumer/reference witness, not the daemon owner candidate.

Affected axes: adjacency improves through exact question-level handoff; C1 depth becomes more precise by separating already-shared rules from source-derived mechanism; siblings gain a reusable boundary without absorbed domain. C2 is untouched. These are **proposed benefits, not measured ordinal improvements**.

Second-order effects: runtime stays read-only (no vacuum/rotate/flush); contract preserves frozen JD count/kill conditions rather than crediting a renamed scope; substrate distinguishes historical shared drop-in values from current manager-derived effective limits; verification distinguishes literal row presence from source correctness, novelty and sibling ownership. A vacuum mention does not establish why a historical portal log vanished, and retained-zero evidence cannot establish event absence.

Risks: equating knowledge overlap with authority would violate mori's explicit boundary; deleting duplicated JD content would silently narrow the frozen claim; treating old config receipts as current would harm freshness. Keep all contradictions explicit and route the ownership question rather than absorbing it. Handoff is **proposed**, not filed/acknowledged/accepted/resolved.

Verification for main agent: compare the pinned mori entry's actual rules to each frozen JD row without changing target/counting predicate; re-read source-correctness reviews and the current merged-config receipt; use the frozen C1 instrument only for its declared predicate, not to settle ownership; obtain mori/integrator response before claiming resolved boundary. This seat executed only enumeration, read-only git grep/show/pin reads, and local text searches; no instruments, tests or builds. No tools installed. No scratch created, therefore none remains.

| mori | `.ufo/waves/round-42/assimilation/validate/corpus/SOURCES.md:166` | adjacent | &#124; S160 &#124; systemd v262 journalctl option grammar and filter application — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/systemd/systemd/v262/src/journal/journalctl.c (companions at the same tag, cited by path only: `src/journal/journalctl-filter.c`, `src/journal/journalctl-util.c`) &#124; code &#124; A &#124; the journalctl read path at the v262 release tag only: how `-b/--boot` takes an optional descriptor and, with none, enables the boot filter with a null boot id and zero offset (help: `Show current boot or the specified boot`), how `-u/--unit` appends its value to the system-unit filter list, how the filter stage resolves a null boot id with zero offset, in the default open with no `--directory`/`--file`/`--root`, to the running machine&#x27;s boot id and then adds a boot-id match plus a conjunction, and how the unit list becomes unit-name matches over the `SYSTEM_UNITS` fields; not the man-page wording (that URL is S122&#x27;s and was rejected as a duplicate), not JSON encoding (S122, S123), not this host&#x27;s journal contents, not journal storage, vacuum or persistence &#124; src/journal/journalctl.c:512-517 (`-b` is `OPTION_OPTIONAL_ARG`; no descriptor sets `arg_boot = true`, `arg_boot_filter = true`, `arg_boot_id = SD_ID128_NULL`, `arg_boot_offset = 0`), :543-544 (`-u` value appended to `arg_system_units`); src/journal/journalctl-filter.c:62-76 (`add_boot`: `add_match_boot_id` then `sd_journal_add_conjunction`), :94-201 (`journal_add_unit_matches`: `add_matches_for_unit_full` plus `sd_journal_add_disjunction` per unit), :203-217 (`add_units` passes `arg_system_units`); src/journal/journalctl-util.c:89-129 (`journal_acquire_boot`: null id and zero offset call `id128_get_boot_for_machine` at :102, `arg_boot_id = boot_id` at :125); src/journal/journalctl-util.h:9-12 (`SYSTEM_UNITS` field list); tag v262 &#124; A descriptorless `-b` restricts the read to the running boot&#x27;s entries and each `-u UNIT` adds that unit&#x27;s messages as journal matches rather than text filters; neither option changes which journal files are opened, and neither proves that any killer acted. &#124; code &#124; A &#124; the journalctl read path at the v262 release tag only: how `-b/--boot` takes an optional descriptor and, with none, enables the boot filter with a null boot id and zero offset (help: `Show current boot or the specified boot`), how `-u/--unit` appends its value to the system-unit filter list, how the filter stage resolves a null boot id with zero offset, in the default open with no `--directory`/`--file`/`--root`, to the running machine&#x27;s boot id and then adds a boot-id match plus a conjunction, and how the unit list becomes unit-name matches over the `SYSTEM_UNITS` fields; not the man-page wording (that URL is S122&#x27;s and was rejected as a duplicate), not JSON encoding (S122, S123), not this host&#x27;s journal contents, not journal storage, vacuum or persistence &#124; src/journal/journalctl.c:512-517 (`-b` is `OPTION_OPTIONAL_ARG`; no descriptor sets `arg_boot = true`, `arg_boot_filter = true`, `arg_boot_id = SD_ID128_NULL`, `arg_boot_offset = 0`), :543-544 (`-u` value appended to `arg_system_units`); src/journal/journalctl-filter.c:62-76 (`add_boot`: `add_match_boot_id` then `sd_journal_add_conjunction`), :94-201 (`journal_add_unit_matches`: `add_matches_for_unit_full` plus `sd_journal_add_disjunction` per unit), :203-217 (`add_units` passes `arg_system_units`); tag v262 &#124; A descriptorless `-b` restricts the read to the running boot&#x27;s entries and each `-u UNIT` adds that unit&#x27;s messages as journal matches rather than text filters; neither option changes which journal files are opened, and neither proves that any killer acted. &#124; code &#124; A &#124; the journalctl read path at the v262 release tag only: how `-b/--boot` takes an optional descriptor and, with none, enables the boot filter with a null boot id and zero offset (help: `Show current boot or the specified boot`), how `-u/--unit` appends its value to the system-unit filter list, how the filter stage resolves a null boot id with zero offset, in the default open with no `--directory`/`--file`/`--root`, to the running machine&#x27;s boot id and then adds a boot-id match plus a conjunction, and how the unit list becomes unit-name matches over the `SYSTEM_UNITS` fields; The order of the daemon&#x27;s own automatic enforcement is therefore not stated by these pages — say &quot;the vacuum command removes the oldest archived files first&quot;, not &quot;the journal&#x27;s reach shrinks from the oldest end&quot;. |

Otherwise, only the latter applies.&quot; (both found, 1×, committed copy). That sentence is the whole public basis for &quot;two journals, two sets of limits&quot;; the staged entry never uses it. |
| mori | `.ufo/waves/round-49/w1/Mo49ScoutStagedJournal.md:79` | adjacent | ### 1.4 `reach-per-journal-second-instance-r48.md` (S554) — REFUSE; its central reading is contradicted by the committed `systemd-journald.service(8)` page |
| mori | `.ufo/waves/round-49/w1/Mo49ScoutStagedJournal.md:87` | same ground | - The **interpretation** does not follow: `systemd-journald.service(8)` (committed, located 1×): &quot;journald will initially use volatile storage, until a call to journalctl --flush (or sending SIGUSR1 to journald) will cause it to switch to persistent logging … This is done automatically on boot via systemd-journal-flush.service&quot;. `journalctl(1)` `--flush`: &quot;Asks the journal daemon to flush any log data stored in /run/log/journal/ into /var/log/journal/, if persistent storage is enabled.&quot; On this host `/usr/lib/systemd/system/systemd-journal-flush.service` exists (`ExecStart=journalctl --flush`, `After=systemd-journald.service`, read-only `grep`). The daemon&#x27;s own boot-0 lines show the runtime journal at 8M at 13:36:19 and the system journal at 45M a second later (13:36:20): the early-boot records moved to `/var`. So an empty `/run/log/journal` is the **post-flush state** of a host with persistent storage; boot 0&#x27;s records are in the system journal (`--list-boots` lists boot 0 with entries from 13:36:19). The runtime journal has &quot;no files&quot; because its records were flushed, not because it has no reach — &quot;a reach of nothing at all&quot; is false; the records of the current boot are reachable. |
| mori | `.ufo/waves/round-49/w1/Mo49ScoutStagedJournal.md:99` | same ground | `source:` row: S560 — `systemd-journald.service(8)` public page. The quote &quot;stores log data either persistently below /var/log/journal or in a volatile way below /run/log/journal/ (in the latter case, it is lost at reboot)&quot; is verified (1×, committed = live). This is the one fully public-supported entry: **supported** for &quot;volatile store is lost at reboot&quot;. |
| mori | `.ufo/waves/round-49/w1/Mo49ScoutStagedJournal.md:102` | same ground | - &quot;the runtime journal is bounded by the boot — its **64M ceiling binds it within a boot**&quot;: the page&#x27;s own next sentences make the volatile store the *early-boot* store, flushed to `/var` automatically (see 1.4); `journald.conf` says the Runtime options apply &quot;during early boot and if the administrator disabled persistent logging&quot;, the System options &quot;if persistent logging is enabled and the system is fully booted up&quot;. On this host the daemon logged `Runtime Journal … is 8M, max 64M` before flush and `System Journal … is 45M, max 256M` after. The staged entry presents the runtime journal as the second journal that serves the boot; per the page it is a transient early-boot buffer on a persistent-storage host, and in-boot records live in the system journal under its own limits. |

| mori | `corpus/SOURCES.md:266` | same ground | &#124; S487 &#124; systemd-journald.service(8) — https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/systemd-journald.service.html &#124; docs &#124; A &#124; where journald keeps log data (persistent under /var/log/journal or volatile under /run/log/journal, lost at reboot), the boot-time default, the flush switch and journald&#x27;s own removal of the oldest archived files; not the retention options (S485), not the journalctl switches (S486) &#124; Description section storage paragraph and the --flush sentence; Files section entry for /var/log/journal; systemd 262 page, copy corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html sha256 a7b443568865bb09f45d1ec83f3a8ad0e6a158d4e2cd21053e72499eb5f51212 &#124; the volatile store is bounded by the boot and the persistent store by size, journald removes the oldest archived files by itself, and a flush moves logging from volatile to persistent — so an empty volatile directory is not proof of lost records &#124; mined &#124; 2026-10-25 &#124; 180d &#124; corpus/knowledge/journal-reach-r49.md &#124; |

href=&quot;#Description&quot;&gt;¶&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;p&gt;These files configure various parameters of the systemd journal service, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:24` | adjacent |     &lt;a href=&quot;systemd-journald.service.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/span&gt;(8)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:27` | adjacent |     for a general description of the syntax.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; instance managing the default namespace is configured by |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:28` | adjacent |     &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/etc/systemd/journald.conf&lt;/code&gt; and associated drop-ins. Instances managing other |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:29` | adjacent |     namespaces read &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/etc/systemd/journald@&lt;em class=&quot;replaceable&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;NAMESPACE&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/em&gt;.conf&lt;/code&gt; |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:32` | adjacent |     &lt;a href=&quot;systemd-journald.service.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/span&gt;(8)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt; |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:77` | same ground |         determined at compilation time), and &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;persistent&lt;/code&gt;&quot; in all others.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Note that journald will initially use volatile storage, until a call to |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:78` | adjacent |         &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;journalctl --flush&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; (or sending &lt;code class=&quot;constant&quot;&gt;SIGUSR1&lt;/code&gt; to journald) will cause |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:87` | adjacent |         &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/var/log/journal/&lt;/code&gt;, as the &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald@.service&lt;/code&gt; service |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:139` | same ground |         those values will override the settings specified here.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/dd&gt;&lt;dt id=&quot;SystemMaxUse=&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemKeepFree=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxFileSize=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxFiles=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;RuntimeMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;RuntimeKeepFree=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;RuntimeMaxFileSize=&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;/span&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;RuntimeMaxFiles=&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;a class=&quot;headerlink&quot; title=&quot;Permalink to this term&quot; href=&quot;#SystemMaxUse=&quot;&gt;¶&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/dt&gt;&lt;dd&gt;&lt;p&gt;Enforce size limits on the journal files |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:155` | adjacent |         &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; ignore all files with |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:159` | adjacent |         calculating current disk usage.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt; and |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:164` | adjacent |         space systemd-journald shall leave free for other uses. |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:165` | adjacent |         &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; will respect both limits |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:171` | adjacent |         systemd-journald is started, the limit will be raised to the |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:175` | adjacent |         journald will stop using more space, but it will not be |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:179` | adjacent |         still be more space used than &lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt; or |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:184` | same ground |         values configured with &lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;RuntimeMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt; capped |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:214` | adjacent |         &lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;SystemMaxUse=&lt;/code&gt; should be sufficient to |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:235` | adjacent |         command line options &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.forward_to_syslog&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:236` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.forward_to_kmsg&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:237` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.forward_to_console&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, and |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:238` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.forward_to_wall&lt;/code&gt;&quot;. If the option name is specified without |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:250` | adjacent |         can be used to receive the forwarded journal entries.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Note: Forwarding is performed synchronously within journald, and may significantly affect its |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:253` | adjacent |         Since journald is implemented as a conventional single-process daemon, forwarding to a completely |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:254` | adjacent |         hung console will block journald. This can have a cascading effect resulting in any services synchronously |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:270` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.max_level_store=&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:271` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.max_level_syslog=&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:272` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.max_level_kmsg=&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:273` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.max_level_console=&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:274` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.max_level_wall=&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:275` | adjacent |         &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd.journald.max_level_socket=&lt;/code&gt;&quot;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a name=&quot;v185&quot;&gt;&lt;/a&gt;Added in version 185.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/dd&gt;&lt;dt id=&quot;ReadKMsg=&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;term&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;varname&quot;&gt;ReadKMsg=&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;a class=&quot;headerlink&quot; title=&quot;Permalink to this term&quot; href=&quot;#ReadKMsg=&quot;&gt;¶&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/dt&gt;&lt;dd&gt;&lt;p&gt;Takes a boolean value. If enabled &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journal&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; processes |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:278` | adjacent |         &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; will turn on kernel auditing on start-up. If disabled it will |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:281` | adjacent |         &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; left it off, it will still collect the generated messages. |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:282` | adjacent |         Defaults to yes in the default journal namespace, and &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;keep&lt;/code&gt;&quot; otherwise.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Note that this option does not control whether &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; collects |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:284` | adjacent |         to prevent &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; from collecting the generated messages, the socket |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:285` | adjacent |         unit &quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;systemd-journald-audit.socket&lt;/code&gt;&quot; can be disabled and, in this case, this setting |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/journald.conf.5.html:320` | adjacent |     &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;refsect1&quot;&gt;&lt;a name=&quot;id-1.9&quot;&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;See Also&quot;&gt;See Also&lt;a class=&quot;headerlink&quot; title=&quot;Permalink to this headline&quot; href=&quot;#See%20Also&quot;&gt;¶&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;simplelist&quot;&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;systemd.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;systemd&lt;/span&gt;(1)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;systemd-journald.service.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/span&gt;(8)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;journalctl.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;journalctl&lt;/span&gt;(1)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;systemd.journal-fields.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;systemd.journal-fields&lt;/span&gt;(7)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, &lt;a href=&quot;systemd-system.conf.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;systemd-system.conf&lt;/span&gt;(5)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;footnotes&quot;&gt;&lt;br&gt;&lt;hr style=&quot;width:100; text-align:left;margin-left: 0&quot;&gt;&lt;div id=&quot;ftn.usr-local-footnote&quot; class=&quot;footnote&quot;&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=&quot;#usr-local-footnote&quot; class=&quot;para&quot;&gt;&lt;sup class=&quot;para&quot;&gt;[1] &lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/a&gt;💣💥🧨💥💥💣 Please note that those configuration files must be available at all times. If |

summary=&quot;Simple list&quot; class=&quot;simplelist&quot;&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.socket&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald-dev-log.socket&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald-audit.socket&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald@.service&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald@.socket&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald-varlink@.socket&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/usr/lib/systemd/systemd-journald&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;div class=&quot;refsect1&quot;&gt;&lt;a name=&quot;id-1.5&quot;&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;h2 id=&quot;Description&quot;&gt;Description&lt;a class=&quot;headerlink&quot; title=&quot;Permalink to this headline&quot; href=&quot;#Description&quot;&gt;¶&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/h2&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/code&gt; is a system service |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:40` | adjacent |     &lt;a href=&quot;journald.conf.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;journald.conf&lt;/span&gt;(5)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to configure |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:41` | same ground |     where log data is placed, independently of the existence of &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/var/log/journal/&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Note that journald will initially use volatile storage, until a call to |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:42` | adjacent |     &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;journalctl --flush&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; (or sending &lt;code class=&quot;constant&quot;&gt;SIGUSR1&lt;/code&gt; to journald) will cause |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:45` | adjacent |     desired (and the default &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;journald.conf&lt;/code&gt; is used), it is sufficient to create the directory, and |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:48` | adjacent |     &lt;a href=&quot;journald.conf.html#&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;citerefentry&quot;&gt;&lt;span class=&quot;refentrytitle&quot;&gt;journald.conf&lt;/span&gt;(5)&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/a&gt; |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:54` | adjacent |     (&quot;&lt;code class=&quot;literal&quot;&gt;\n&lt;/code&gt;&quot;, ASCII &lt;code class=&quot;constant&quot;&gt;10&lt;/code&gt;) and &lt;code class=&quot;constant&quot;&gt;NUL&lt;/code&gt; bytes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/code&gt; is stopped, the stream connections associated with all |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:66` | adjacent |     &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/code&gt; stores copies of the file descriptors for those streams in |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:67` | adjacent |     the service manager. If &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/code&gt; is restarted using |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:71` | adjacent |     restart &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/code&gt;, but stopping it is not recommended.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Note that the log record metadata for records transferred via such standard output/error streams reflect the |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:78` | adjacent |     line tool.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Currently, the number of parallel log streams &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/code&gt; will accept is limited to |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:83` | adjacent |     stream managed by its own instance of &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;. Namespaces are independent of |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:85` | adjacent |     exists, managed by &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald.service&lt;/code&gt; (and its associated socket |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:87` | adjacent |     &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;systemd-journald@.service&lt;/code&gt; service template. The instance name is the namespace |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:97` | adjacent |     is associated with the namespace.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;By default, only the default namespace will collect kernel and audit log messages.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The &lt;span class=&quot;command&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;systemd-journald&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt; instance of the default namespace is configured through |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:98` | adjacent |     &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/etc/systemd/journald.conf&lt;/code&gt; (see below), while the other instances are configured |
| mori | `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/systemd-journald.service.8.html:99` | adjacent |     through &lt;code class=&quot;filename&quot;&gt;/etc/systemd/journald@&lt;em class=&quot;replaceable&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;NAMESPACE&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/em&gt;.conf&lt;/code&gt;. The journal |

| mori | `corpus/knowledge/journal-reach-r49.md:14` | same ground | Reach is how far back a mori read of the journal can look. The rows below give two bounds of different kinds, plus a time rule that is off by default. The **persistent** store is bounded by **size**: the use limits cap the space it may take, and both journald&#x27;s automatic removal and the vacuum switches take the **oldest archived** files first and never an active file. The **volatile** store is bounded by the **boot**: its data is lost at reboot. `MaxRetentionSec=` is a third, separate time rule whose default of 0 turns it off. |
| mori | `corpus/knowledge/journal-reach-r49.md:32` | same ground | &#124; A size vacuum removes the oldest archived files until the space they use falls below the requested size. &#124; S486 &#124; `--vacuum-size= removes the oldest archived journal files until the disk space they use falls below the specified size` &#124; |
| mori | `corpus/knowledge/journal-reach-r49.md:38` | same ground | &#124; A flush switches journald from volatile to persistent logging. &#124; S487 &#124; `a call to journalctl --flush (or sending SIGUSR1 to journald) will cause it to switch to persistent logging` &#124; |
| mori | `corpus/knowledge/journal-reach-r49.md:39` | same ground | &#124; journald itself removes the oldest archived files to limit disk use, apart from any operator vacuum. &#124; S487 &#124; `systemd-journald will automatically remove the oldest archived journal files to limit disk use` &#124; |

| mori | `corpus/knowledge/journal-reach-r49.md:48` | adjacent | Everything numeric here is the receipt `corpus/evidence/sources-r49/host-journal-reach.txt` of that day, not a standing value. The drop-in names a 256M system and a 64M runtime ceiling; the capture shows 56M in use in total (19M system, 37M user), so on that day the 256M ceiling was not what bounded the reach — the store held two boots, the older one from 2026-10-24 20:57. Two readings in the receipt must not be merged: the daemon&#x27;s boot-time line puts the system journal at 45M and the later `--system --disk-usage` reports 19M. They count different things (a directory at boot against files the command can see), the receipt records no cause, and no row above rests on either the 45M or the 8M runtime line, because those lines came from a unit-scoped message search (`journalctl -b 0 -u systemd-journald -g …`) that the refusal row S351 asked the flip to avoid; the receipt keeps them as captured, and this entry does not use them. |

[INFERENCE] The fall from 269M to 56M is consistent with that vacuum; the entry states no cause as fact. |

Journal record 1 is the invocation&#x27;s version line `earlyoom 1.9.0` at 42000000, exactly ActiveEnterTimestampMonotonic=42000000, so it is inside the scope (round-26/contract.md:78-79: scope is the current invocation&#x27;s lines in the current boot whose __MONOTONIC_TIMESTAMP is &gt;= ActiveEnterTimestampMonotonic; the version line is main.c:161 `fprintf(stderr, \&quot;earlyoom \&quot; VERSION \&quot;\\n\&quot;)` at earlyoom v1.9.0). Journal record 2, `Suppressed 42 messages from earlyoom.service` at 42001234, is also inside the scope, so scope_complete is false (round-26/contract.md:92-93 defines scope_complete as true only when the version line of the current invocation is inside the scope; A2-01 adds the in-scope suppressed line as a second disqualifier). No `sending SIG... to process` line and no escalation line is in scope and oomd_notices is 0, so there is no positive evidence (round-26/contract.md:97-98); an active earlyoom with scope_complete false is KILLERS_UNKNOWN exit 2 (round-26/contract.md:99-101). earlyoom.state is active because the show text carries ActiveState=active (round-26/contract.md:91; the same mapping frozen in cases.json c01/c04/c13). earlyoom.version is 1.9.0 because the version line is in scope (round-26/contract.md:82 with :78-79). earlyoom.dryrun is false because no `dryrun mode enabled` line is in scope and the output spec gives dryrun no null alternative (round-26/contract.md:89 and :94; frozen cases.json c01/c19). oomd.state inactive and oomd.notices_this_boot 0 follow the oomd show text and the oomd_notices input (round-26/contract.md:95; frozen cases.json c01/c06). Deliberately not pinned: earlyoom.grammar, because round-26/contract.md:91 lists the two grammar values but states no condition for choosing between them, and the two readings that fit the frozen set (verified when the in-scope version line reads 1.9.0, versus verified only for a complete scope) disagree exactly for this input; earlyoom.actions, because frozen case c05 declines to pin the counts of an active reader whose scope is incomplete; earlyoom.thresholds, because round-26/contract.md:92 gives thresholds a null alternative and frozen case c04, also an incomplete scope, leaves thresholds unpinned; earlyoom.earlier_invocation_send_lines, because the contract does not decide its value for a boot with no earlier invocation.&quot;, |
